# Gender Action Plan

GGC is committed to ensuring that its internal capacities and external (institutional- and project-specific) approaches to enhancing gender equality are aligned with the GCF's requirements.

This is reflected in the below Gender Action Plan.


| Activities | Indicators and Targets | Timeline | Responsibilities and Costs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Impact assessment, in modeling significant phase shift capital investments and associated social and environmental impacts across the GGC portfolio, on the basis of its ability to support greater gender equality and women's empowerment within the target countries and sectors, through: | Improved access to clean energy and empowerment of local communities, including women, girls, and vulnerable populations, through renewable energy, energy efficiency, enabling energy efficiency and new technologies. This includes a focus on increasing the number of girls living in cities and working in industries to make positive health-behavioural shifts through the provision of better technologies and energy efficient designs to help reduce energy use in urban contexts | Improved access to safe, reliable and affordable low-emission transport systems for local communities, with special attention paid to the needs of those in vulnerable populations, including women and girls, through improved public transport solutions. | Improved livelihoods and enhances the resilience of vulnerable communities, including women and girls, to the negative climate impacts currently affecting critical agricultural and food systems. |
| Impact assessment, in line-with energy, recreation, and economic activities for women and girls through enhancements to the built and natural environments that they rely on (ranging from improvements in WASH and energy infrastructure, through to improvements in land-use and conservation of forests and ecosystems). | Improved access to safe, reliable and affordable low-emission transport systems for local communities, with special attention paid to the needs of those in vulnerable populations, including women and girls, through improved public transport solutions. | Improved livelihoods and enhances the resilience of vulnerable communities, including women and girls, to the negative climate impacts currently affecting critical agricultural and food systems. |
| Outcome Statement: By demonstrating how the provision of green guarantees can support widespread roll-out of inclusive climate change mitigation and adaptation projects, the GGC will achieve the following gender-responsive outcomes (with sex disaggregated against both field impacts at or near, gender parity): | Enhanced access to clean energy for communities, including women and children, through GHG emissions reductions and adaptation measures relating to both built and natural environments. | More equitable decision-making between men and women, reduction in gender-based violence, increased access to and control over resources, and economic empowerment of women and girls through the GGC's Environmental and Social Management System (ESMS), mandatory project safeguards. |

# Stakeholder Consultation Framework

## Establishing Institutional Processes and Systems to Enhance Gender Equality Outcomes

### Dedicated Necessary Finances and Human Resources to Implement This 100% Annual Compliance with Established in Year 1 Consulting Team Appointed by GGC to Head EGG on Gender Action Plan, with Indicator Data Disaggregated by Sex to Ensure Accountability

- Dedicate necessary human and financial resources to build, strengthen and sustain GGC's capacity related to gender equality, including:
    - Line items for GGC's annual budgets to cover processes and systems (e.g., GGC's years in operation).
    - Cost covers the ongoing engagement of the GGC's gender focal point, as well as project-specific gender expertise, as required.
- ESM System: Policy and Frameworks to be established in Year 1.

### Costs Included in the GGC's Annual Budgets, as Followed (see also page 115)

- Dedicated gender expertise within the GGC - In-year funding for ongoing gender, climate & EAS resources (T). The team will consist of one (1) resource person (supported by external consultants) and grow year-on-year to ultimately provide seven (7) resources (split equally between gender, climate and EAS).
- Project-level consultancy services, which includes the cost of in-country experts to be mobilized on

# Output 2: Ensure project-level gender assessments and action plans (SAPs) are developed

(NOTE: Project-level gender assessments and specific gender indicators and targets can only be established as and when projects that the GGC will support are identified. Thus, indicators and targets are identified where shown below.)

As part of each project's gender assessment, ensure:

- Relevant sex-disaggregated data at a country level is collected and analyzed
- Women’s organizations working on gender issues are identified and engaged
- Gender-related baseline data specific to the sector and country is established

The project is aligned with / in support of the country’s SDG, NDC and policy gender-related commitments

Contextualized vulnerabilities (and opportunities) are identified (using the Sector and Country Assessments in Annex 8.1 as a basis for further research and engagement)

Operationalize strategies to address these vulnerabilities are well defined and adequately integrated into the project design and delivery processes (using Table 6, Annex 8.1, as a basis for further research and engagement)

# Enhanced institutional capacity of project owners to deliver gender-responsive support that leads to greater security outcomes

## Context B: Enhanced institutional capacity of project owners to deliver gender-responsive support that leads to greater security outcomes

### Guarantees provided to women-led businesses and / or targeted allocation of guarantees to projects that explicitly look to address gaps in financing for women.

At least 20% of all projects comply with one or more of the ZISCChallenge Financing for Women criteria:

- Women-led or owned women businesses
- Women gain access to guarantees from the GGC

### Project Owners capacitated to develop and deliver gender-responsive support through technical assistance (TA) from the GGC

Number of Project Owners who receive technical assistance from the GGC to enhance / improve the gender-responsiveness of their project design

In perpetuity (duration of GGC’s years in operation):

- Project Owners with data verified by GGC's consulting firm
  - AE to monitor and report on progress.
  - Costs: M&E budget items part of the GGC's overarching M&E budget for 2021-32 (£US$5,603,907.00 total) (See Annex 11).

In perpetuity (duration of GGC’s years in operation):

- Number of Project Owners who receive technical assistance from the GGC to enhance / improve the gender-responsiveness of their project design
  - AE to monitor and report on progress.
  - Costs: M&E budget items part of the GGC's overarching M&E budget for 2021-32 (£US$5,603,907.00 total) (See Annex 11).
